stdout | test/authn/preauth-policy.test.ts > the pre-auth class is narrow, and structurally so > no pre-auth route has a subject parameter naming another user
      · 8 pre-auth routes, none with a subject parameter: POST /organizations/:organizationId/authn/sign-in, POST /organizations/:organizationId/authn/sign-out, POST /organizations/:organizationId/authn/mfa/enrolment, POST /organizations/:organizationId/authn/mfa/enrolment/confirmation, POST /organizations/:organizationId/authn/mfa/challenge, POST /organizations/:organizationId/authn/activation, POST /organizations/:organizationId/authn/password-reset, POST /organizations/:organizationId/authn/password-reset/completion

stdout | test/authn/preauth-policy.test.ts > the pre-auth class is narrow, and structurally so > every pre-auth route declares a stage from the closed set and a written reason
      · POST /organizations/:organizationId/authn/sign-in -> preauth/anonymous (Sign-in is what produces a principal; there is nothing yet for a capability to be evaluated against. The credential check IS the verification.)
      · POST /organizations/:organizationId/authn/sign-out -> preauth/session-any (Sign-out acts only on the presented session and must work for one that has not satisfied its challenge — a half-authenticated session that cannot be ended is a session that has to expire instead.)
      · POST /organizations/:organizationId/authn/mfa/enrolment -> preauth/session-partial (Enrolling one's own second factor is a step of authentication, not a tenant action: an elevated role cannot complete sign-in until it has enrolled, so requiring a capability here would be a cycle. The subject is always the session holder.)
      · POST /organizations/:organizationId/authn/mfa/enrolment/confirmation -> preauth/session-partial (Confirms the enrolment started on this session with a valid code. Same reasoning as the enrolment route; the subject is the session holder.)
      · POST /organizations/:organizationId/authn/mfa/challenge -> preauth/session-partial (The second factor of a sign-in in progress. The session it marks is the one that presented it; there is no parameter naming another.)
      · POST /organizations/:organizationId/authn/activation -> preauth/anonymous (Activation is performed by somebody who has no account yet, holding a single-use token. Requiring a session would make the flow impossible.)
      · POST /organizations/:organizationId/authn/password-reset -> preauth/anonymous (Requested by somebody who cannot sign in. The response is identical whatever the address turns out to be.)
      · POST /organizations/:organizationId/authn/password-reset/completion -> preauth/anonymous (Completed by somebody who cannot sign in, holding a single-use token from a namespace that is not the invitation namespace.)

stdout | test/authn/preauth-policy.test.ts > the pre-auth class is narrow, and structurally so > every registered route has a policy, and the four kinds are the only ones
      · 130 registered routes; policy kinds in use: capability, preauth, public
